Association setup gets an association setup response (type 6). Malformed datagrams are silently dropped (no crash).\n\nNow I should design the PoC:\n1. Start the service with a dynamically generated config in a temp dir.\n2. Wait for readiness — either poll the UDP port with heartbeat requests until a reply, or watch stdout for \"listening for new PFCP connections\". Simpler: loop sending heartbeat requests until reply or timeout.\n3. Send a sequence of probes (heartbeat req, association setup req, malformed datagrams) to exercise input handling.\n4. Observe: did the process crash? Did we get replies?\n5. After observation window, terminate (SIGTERM — the service handles it gracefully, rc 0). Report `timed_out: true`, signal null.\n\nWait — on termination via proc.terminate() the rc was 0 (graceful shutdown via signal handler). The contract says: \"returncode = the exit code produced by your termination, signal = null\". Good.\n\nIf it crashed: kind=crash_trace, value = panic trace from stderr, signal name e.g. SIGSEGV (Go panic would exit with code 2, not a signal; but handle both).\n\nNote: subprocess returncode is negative for signal death; convert to signal name via signal.Signals(-rc).\n\nAlso need to handle readiness failure: error = \"listener did not become ready\".\n\nAlso `observable.path` — set null.\n\nDeterministic probe: fixed sequence of datagrams. Bounded rounds: e.g., a handful of probes with timeouts. `--timeout` controls overall observation window.\n\nEdge cases: the process might die at startup (e.g., bad config parse). Then error might still be null — the run completed, process exited with a returncode. Hmm — if the process exits before we can probe, is that a run failure? It's the observed behavior; but we couldn't interact. I'd report it faithfully: returncode set, signal null, observable kind=protocol_transcript value \"no_response\", error null? Actually if the listener never became ready because process exited, that's a genuine failure of the run (\"a listener that never became ready\" is listed as genuine failure). Hmm, but with the correct binary it will become ready. For other binaries/args, error \"listener did not become ready\" is reasonable. But careful: if process crashed during startup due to our config... our config is known-good. I'll set error=\"listener did not become ready\" only if readiness polling fails AND process hasn't exited; if process exited, that's also a genuine failure? The spec example lists \"a listener that never became ready\" as a genuine failure. But then the crash case: if our probe crashes the service, that's observable crash, not error.\n\nPlan: \n- readiness phase: send heartbeat probes every 200ms up to readiness timeout (min(timeout,10s) or so). If we get any reply → ready.\n- If process exited during readiness → this is actually the observed behavior; but we couldn't interact... I'll treat: if process exits during readiness with a crash-like output, still report faithfully with error=\"service exited before listener became ready\". Hmm, that conflicts with \"report observed outcome faithfully\". Actually if it exited on its own without interaction, the run itself failed to interact — but the observed outcome (exit) is still reported. I think setting error is fine per spec's example of \"a listener that never became ready\".\n- probe phase: send deterministic sequence of datagrams; collect replies (hex). Brief settle window; check if process died.\n- If process died: capture output; if returncode negative → signal name; kind=crash_trace with value = stderr+stdout tail (panic trace). Also if it died from signal.\n- Else: kind=protocol_transcript, value = transcript string of probes+replies (hex) or \"no_response\".\n- Terminate with SIGTERM, wait, then SIGKILL if needed.\n- timed_out = true if we had to terminate it.\n\nAlso check binary exists at start → error if missing, returncode null, etc.\n\nFor crash detection, a Go panic exits with status 2 and prints \"panic:\" to stderr. A signal death gives negative returncode. crash_trace value: full crash trace text — use combined stderr (panic goes to stderr). I'll extract from first \"panic:\" occurrence if present, else full stderr.\n\nTranscript format: lines like \"probe[0] sent=<hex> reply=<hex>|no_response\". value as a single string.\n\nLet me also double check determinism: replies contain timestamps (recovery timestamp IE ee46a292 varies). That's fine — we report what we observe.\n\nTimeout semantics: `--timeout <seconds>` = overall observation window after start. Default maybe 10s.\n\nLet me write poc.py now."}}
